Let's design final code structure:
Top-level helpers:

   Sort indices by h descending. Choose counts c_l for levels sorted descending such that sum c_l*level_l = target. Need counts sum n. This is a bounded knapsack small. For levels 0,0.5,1: need c1=c0. So set c0= floor or ceil? If n even target n/2. Let m = n - c1 - c0 even? Sum = c1*1 + c0*0 +0.5*m =0.5n +0.5(c1-c0). Need c1=c0. Choose c1=c0=k. Local search functions:
1. `active_gradient_from_corr(h, corr, top_count, alpha)`:
   - n=h.size; center=n-1.

2. `grad_update_step(h, g, target, steps)`:
   - d = -g; d -= mean(d).
   - norm = l2(d); if small return.
   - d /= norm.
   - For eta in steps: cand=project_density(h + eta*d, target); c=eval; if c<best return.
   - Maybe also try normalized by gradient max: scale * eta.
   Steps: We can use candidate directions not just gradient but also random. Use a set of eta values scaled by h scale: direction norm=1, values changes up to eta. We can include (0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5). Projecting to sum/box limits.
   - If none, try `-g` and `+g`? Maybe gradient sign.
3. `pair_search_continuous(h, deadline, top_count, alpha)`:
   - Iterate: compute corr, top, gradient. Determine candidate positions.
   - Use arrays of gradients; pos_inc candidates = indices with smallest g where h<1; pos_dec = largest g where h>0. Try all combos (maybe 10x10). Move mass d from j (decrease) to i (increase): cap=min(1-h[i],h[j]). For fractions of cap (0.25,0.5,0.75,1). Evaluate. Accept best improvement.
   - If no improvement, random pairs (e.g. 100 per iteration) with random d. Or use simulated annealing.

   - h as int8/16.
   - `eval_bin(h)` returns peak int and maybe corr int.

   - We can maintain current_c, current_peak. For candidate, we can first evaluate a cheap partial? Maybe full.
   - Propose indices:
      * Compute corr full int. `top_idx` by corr (max counts). For each top idx, compute gradient as for continuous (with h float) or directly conceptual. But for binary, gradient coefficients from top lags are mostly integers. We can use same `active_gradient_from_corr` with h_float? It requires h float and corr counts. Convert h_bin to float. Candidate increase = low g and h=0; decrease = high g and h=1.
      * Also directly from active lag: if s>0, bad pairs where h[i]=1,h[i-s]=0. Choose i from one, and j from a pair with opposite orientation maybe. But gradient easier.
   - Try candidate swaps: pos_inc (h=0 with min g) and pos_dec (h=1 with max g). Also random. Evaluate full int. Accept if peak lower -> c lower. For annealing, accept if peak same/lower with probability temp.
   - Since objective integer, we can use `score = 2*peak/n` and allow small worse.

Let's formulate active polynomial:
```
def polish_active(h, deadline, top_count=max(16,int(n*0.05)), alpha=4.0, min_tol=1e-12, max_iter=...):
  best=project(h,target); best_c=eval(best); current=best.copy(); current_c=best_c
  stall=0
  rng=...
  for it in range(max_iter):
    if time>deadline: break
    corr=full_corr(current)
    top_count = max(min_k, min(int(n*top_frac), 2n-1))
    top_idx = argpartition(corr, -top_count)[-top_count:]
    top_vals=corr[top_idx]
    # weight maybe softmax over top_vals with temperature = (max-min)/10 or 1e-4? If values in counts ~e.g.90, differences small. Use alpha=100?
    dv=top_vals - top_vals.min()
    w=np.exp(alpha*dv); w/=w.sum()  # if dv small, uniform
    g=0
    for idx,wt in zip(top_idx,w): accumulate
    d=-g; d-=mean(d); normalize
    # try step values scaled by something: we can estimate gradient scale. Use step_values = [1e-4,3e-4,1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,0.1]
    improved=False
    for step in step_values: cand=project(current+step*d,target); c=eval; if c<current_c-1e-13: current=cand; current_c=c; improved=True; break
    # Also try multiple sign? if d maybe wrong due active weights? Could also try -d? But gradient should decrease C if weights positive. Yet because objective non-smooth and projection maybe. We can evaluate first candidate both +d and -d? Actually d=-g. Try `current + step*d` and `current - step*d`. If one works.
    if not improved:
       # pair moves
       ...
    if current_c < best_c: best=...
    if current_c < best_c - 1e-13: stall=0 else stall++
    if stall>100: add random perturbation: current=project(best+normal(0,0.01),target)
```
Pair moves:
- Find `g` (already). Positive positions to increase: indices where h<1 and g low (since d=-g high). Negative to decrease: h>0 and g high. We can get `inc_pool = np.argsort(g)[:20]` (lowest g) but among h<1; `dec_pool = np.argsort(-g)[:20]` among h>0. For each pair, move t from dec to inc: cap=min(1-h[inc],h[dec]); try t fractions [0.01,0.05,0.2,0.5]. Evaluate. Accept best pair improvement (not first? To save time, evaluate several and choose best). This can make moves more targeted than average direction.
- For binary rounding, if h values near 0/1, cap small.

Random perturbation: If stall, current = project(best + rng.normal(0,sigma),target). sigma adaptive e.g. 0.01, 0.02. Then run gradient.

This is essentially previous but with longer and smaller steps.

Binary optimizer design in detail:
Input h_float, n. We can create binary h_int by thresholding: `b = (h_float >= 0.5).astype(int16)` and adjust count to n//2 by selecting th closest to 0.5? Better:
```
def to_binary(h):
  n=h.size; m=n//2
  idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
  b=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.int16)
  b[idx[:m]]=1
  return b
```
This ensures sum m. But if h has not exactly median, okay. Could also try soft threshold at 0.4/0.5/0.6.

Objective for binary:
```
def corr_bin(b):
  a=1-b
  return np.correlate(b.astype(np.int32?) , a.astype(...), mode='full')
```

The resulting max of linear functions plus constant is convex in h? Actually product approximated by tangent: h_i h_j ≈ h_i g_j + h_j g_i - g_i g_j. This affine. Then problem min max of affine + box/sum is an LP. We can solve LP (CVXPY/ECOS) to get a new h, then nonlinear. This is a **linearized Majorization Minimization (MM)** for nonconvex? Since product not convex, tangent may be above or below? For bilinear, tangent is not valid. But we can use it heuristically: minimize conservative? Let's consider optimizing in **frequency domain**. The lags k small dominate. For nonperiodic finite, we can approximate by assuming h smooth and boundary taper. The objective for small lags is related to derivative: F(t)=∫h(1-h-t h')? General expansion: F(t)=C0 - t∫ h h' + O(t²) = C0 + t/2 (if h endpoints 0,1?) But if h(0),h(2) not extremes? `project_density(x, target)`:
```
x = np.asarray(x,float).copy()
x = np.nan_to_num(...)
x = np.clip(x,0,1)
n=x.size
if n==0: return x
# Use bisection for shift to reach target
s=np.sum(x)
if abs(s-target)<1e-12: return x
lo,hi=-1,1
for _ in range(70):
 mid=(lo+hi)/2
 s=np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0,1))
 if s<target: lo=mid else hi=mid
h=np.clip(x+0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
# adjust residual exactly by distributing to free
res=target-np.sum(h)
for _ in range(3):
 if abs(res)<1e-14: break
 free=np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12))[0]
 if free.size==0:
   # if all 0 or1, perturb one to 1e-12?
   free=np.where((h>0)&(h<1))[0]
   if free.size==0:
      # adjust extreme carefully
      if res>0 and np.any(h<1): free=np.where(h<1)[0]
      elif res<0 and np.any(h>0): free=np.where(h>0)[0]
 if free.size:
   # distribute to a few random? deterministic first free
   # To avoid exceeding, distribute res by chunks
   # simple: add res/free.size, clip, repeat
   h[free] += res / free.size
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   res=target-np.sum(h)
# final single adjustment
res=target-np.sum(h)
if abs(res)>1e-15:
  # find best index (not at bounds)
  free=np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12))[0]
  if free.size:
     i=free[0]
     h[i]+=res
  else:
     # adjust an index with enough capacity, may clip; then compensate another
     if res>0:
        i=np.argmin(h) # value 0? add
     else:
        i=np.argmax(h) # value1? subtract
     h[i]+=res
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
# final res distribute among all with capacity
res=target-np.sum(h)
if abs(res)>1e-13:
  # choose up to n indices toward res sign
  if res>0:
    idx=np.argsort(h)[:max(1,int(abs(res)*2)+2)]
  else:
    idx=np.argsort(-h)[:max(1,int(abs(res)*2)+2)]
  for i in idx:
    add = res if len(idx)==1 else min(res, (1-h[i]) if res>0 else (h[i]))? Better loop until.
```
Simpler: use previous method with one free. For target = n/2, residual machine small. We can do:
```
res=target - sum(h)
if res !=0:
  if res>0:
    idx=np.where(h<1)[0]
    # add in chunks of up to 1-h[i]
  else similar
```
We can implement a helper `fix_sum(h,target)` robust.

`fix_sum`:
```
def fix_sum(h,target):
 h=np.asarray(h,float).copy(); h=np.clip(h,0,1)
 for _ in range(10):
   res=target-np.sum(h)
   if abs(res)<1e-14: break
   if res>0:
      idx=np.where(h<1.0)[0]
      if idx.size==0: break
      # sort by h ascending to use lowest
      idx=idx[np.argsort(h[idx])[:min(len(idx),res*2+10)]]
   else:
      resn=-res
      idx=np.where(h>0.0)[0]
      idx=idx[np.argsort(-h[idx])[:min(len(idx),resn*2+10)]]
   # adjust one by one
   rem=res
   for i in idx:
     if abs(rem)<1e-15: break
     if rem>0:
        add=min(rem,1.0-h[i])
        h[i]+=add; rem-=add
     else:
        sub=min(-rem,h[i])
        h[i]-=sub; rem+=sub
 return h
```
This loops possibly many if res large; but residual small after bisection. In initial project, bisection gets close. Good.

`evaluate_corr(h)`:
```
def evaluate_corr(h):
  n=h.size
  if n==0: return np.zeros(0), float('inf')
  a=np.asarray(h,float)
  b=1.0-a
  return np.correlate(a,b,'full'), n
```
`evaluate_c5_from_corr(corr,n)`: dx=2/n; max(corr)*dx. Use float.

For performance, in local search we can compute corr and then c directly. `full_c(h, n_cache)` maybe.

`active_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha, min_top=1)`:
```
def active_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha):
 n=h.size; center=n-1; m=corr.size
 top_count=min(max(top_count,1),m)
 # Use argpartition if top_count < m
 if top_count == m: idx=np.arange(m)
 else:
   # Need largest. np.argpartition(corr, m-top_count)[-top_count:]
   idx=np.argpartition(corr, m-top_count)[-top_count:]
 vals=corr[idx]
 # If vals finite
 # softmax: use temp. Let range=vals.max()-vals.min(); if range<1e-12: uniform; else alpha_eff = alpha / max(range,1e-6)? Wait corr counts values e.g. 90; range maybe 1. To make weights emphasize differences, use alpha * (vals - vals.max()) no scaling? If alpha default 10, range 2 => exp up to 1000? maybe okay. Use `dv=vals-vals.min(); w=np.exp(alpha*dv / max(dv.max()+1e-12,1e-6))`? Then max weight 1, min exp(-alpha). If alpha=10, range>1, min negligible. But if we want balance, alpha small. We'll pass alpha=5 and not normalize? Use `dv=vals-vals.max()` clipped, w=exp(alpha*dv)` (max 1). If alpha=5, differences 0.1 -> weight ratio e^-0.5=0.6. Good. Fracs: if range large, top dominates.
 w = np.exp(alpha * (vals - vals.max())); w = np.clip(w,1e-12,1); w/=w.sum
 g=np.zeros(n)
 for t,wt in zip(idx,w):
   if wt<1e-12: continue
   s=int(t)-center
   if s==0:
     g += wt*(1.0-2.0*h)
   elif s>0:
     lag=s
     # first term for i=lag..n-1: 1
     g[lag:] += wt
     # - h[i-lag] for i=lag..n-1
     g[lag:] -= wt*h[:n-lag]
     # - h[i+lag] for i=0..n-1-lag
     g[:n-lag] -= wt*h[lag:]
   else:
     lag=-s
     g[:n-lag] += wt
     g[:n-lag] -= wt*h[lag:]
     g[lag:] -= wt*h[:n-lag]
 return g
```
Note weights normalized; sum w=1, so gradient magnitude ~dx? Not include dx. For line search, scale arbitrary. We can multiply by 2/n? Not needed.

Potential issue: If using top_count too small, gradient from only max lag. We can set top_count maybe max(8, int(0.05*n)) but for n=1800 top=90 gradient loop heavy. Use `top_count = max(8, min(64, int(0.04*n)))`. For n=450, 18; n=900,36; n=1800,64.

`direction_step`:
```
def step_from_gradient(h,g,target,steps):
 d=-g; d-=np.mean(d)
 nrm=np.linalg.norm(d)
 if nrm<1e-14: return h,None
 d/d=nrm
 return d
```
In polish loop, evaluate both plus/minus? Since d=-g; moving +d should decrease. But if active set weights include max, subgradient of max points to face; yes. We can also try `d` and `-d` to guard sign, but -d would increase max likely; still maybe due nonconvex? Not needed.

Use steps: [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]. Since projection may limit. If no improvement, try smaller [0.005,0.01]. But evaluating 8 candidates each iteration okay. We can order: 0.05,0.1,0.2,0.02,0.05? We want find any. Use a dynamic step based on previous success.

`active_polish(h, deadline, top_count, alpha, max_iter, sigma_rew...)`:
We'll make a generic function returning best. It will also call pair moves if gradient fails.

Pseudo:
```
def active_polish(h, deadline, top_count=None, alpha=3.0, max_iter=2000, pair_pool=8):
 target=0.5*h.size
 best=project_density(h,target); best_c=eval_c5(best)
 cur=best.copy(); cur_c=best_c
 rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
 stall=0
 step=0.1
 for it in range(max_iter):
   if time>=deadline: break
   corr=evaluate_corr(cur)[0]
   if top_count is None: top_count=max(8,min(48,int(0.05*cur.size)))
   g=active_gradient(cur,corr,top_count,alpha)
   d=-g; d-=mean; norm...
   if norm>0:
      d/=norm
      tvals = tuple([step*0.25, step*0.5, step, step*2]) maybe plus 0.02,0.05
      # ensure unique
      best_cand=None
      for t in tvals:
        cand=project_density(cur + t*d, target)
        cc=eval_c5(cand)
        if cc < cur_c - 1e-13:
           cur=cand; cur_c=cc; best=...; stall=0; step*=1.2; break
      if improved: continue
   # If didn't improve, pair moves
   # Use g from above.
   inc_all = np.argsort(g)  # increasing reduces
   dec_all = np.argsort(-g)
   inc_pool = []
   for idx in inc_all[:pair_pool*3]:
     if cur[idx] < 1.0-1e-8: inc_pool.append(idx)
     if len>=pair_pool: break
   dec_pool similarly h>1e-8
   improved=False
   for i in inc_pool:
     for j in dec_pool:
       if i==j: continue
       cap=min(1-cur[i],cur[j])
       if cap<1e-10: continue
       for frac in (0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0):
         cand=cur.copy(); cand[i]+=frac*cap; cand[j]-=frac*cap
         cc=eval_c5(cand)
         if cc < cur_c -1e-13:
            cur=cand; cur_c=cc; improved=True; break
       if improved: break
     if improved: break
   if improved:
      best...
      stall=0
      step = max(0.02, step*0.8?)
   else:
      stall +=1
      # occasionally random
      if stall in (10,20,30,50,80) or stall%50==0:
         sig = 0.005*min(1, stall/50)+0.001
         cur=project_density(best + rng.normal(0,sig,cur.size), target)
         cur_c=eval_c5(cur)
      if stall>150:
         cur=best.copy(); cur_c=best_c; step=max(0.01,step*0.5)
         if step<1e-5: break
   if cur_c < best_c:
      best=cur.copy(); best_c=cur_c
 return best,best_c
```

```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1300):
 start=time.perf_counter()
 deadline=start+budget_s-10
 rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
 best_h=None; best_c=inf; best_n=0
 def record(h): project, eval, update nonlocal? no closures. Use helper returning tuple? In run, just code.
 # get parent
 parent = None
 try: raw=np.asarray(initial_h_values,float); if raw.ndim==1 and raw.size>=2: parent=... clip
 except: parent=None
 if parent is not None:
   n0=parent.size
   h0=project_density(parent,0.5*n0)
   best_h=h0; best_c=eval_c5(h0); best_n=n0
 else create sin n=450.
 # allocate
 # Phase A: quick polish parent at n0 (time 0.45*budget maybe?)
 rem=deadline-time
 allocA=0.35*rem
 hA,cA=active_polish(best_h, start+allocA, top_count=?, alpha=4, max_iter=...)
 record
 # Phase B: ternary/binary starts from best_h, each short polish (remaining 0.25)
 n0=best_h.size
 variants=[]
 b1=to_binary_rank(best_h); variants.append(b1)
 for k in [n0//4, n0//2, n0//3, int(0.6*n0/2?)]: variants.append(ternary(best_h,k))
 # Maybe if parent n0 not 450 but 450, okay.
 for v in variants:
   allocB = (0.25*rem)/len(variants)
   hB,cB=active_polish(v, dl, top_count small, max_iter)
   record
 # Phase C: high res n1=2*best_n (up to 900/1800). If time >150
   n1=min(2*best_n, 1800?) maybe 900 if best_n=450. If best_n smaller maybe 900.
   h1=upsample_h(best_h,n1)
   record (upsampled may lower? Could be higher due interpolation? Evaluate)
   c1=active_polish(h1,...)
   record
   # binary at n1 from best_h and polish? maybe active_polish on to_binary_rank(h1)
 # Phase D: if time, n2=2*n1 up to 3600? Maybe just quick active_polish
 # Final polish best_h with remaining time using active_polish (long, smaller steps)
 # Ensure all projected
 return best_h,best_c,best_n
```

Top_count/alpha tuning:
- For active_polish, we can pass `alpha` based on n: small n alpha=8, large alpha=4. Use weights over top values normalized by range. Let's implement weights robustly:
```
def softmax_lag_weights(vals, alpha):
   if len==0: return
   v=vals.astype(float)
   maxv=v.max(); minv=v.min(); rng=maxv-minv
   if rng < 1e-12: w=np.full_like(v,1/len)
   else:
      # scale so max weight 1, min ratio exp(-alpha)
      w=np.exp(alpha*(v-maxv)/rng)
      w/=w.sum()
   return w
```
If alpha large, top lag dominates; if alpha=1, uniform among top. We can switch with iterations: initially alpha=2 (balance), later alpha=10 (focus). In active_polish, alpha parameter maybe default 4. Could periodically reduce/increase.

Could we improve by directly optimizing the **active lag set** using a quadratic trust region? At current h, for active lags with weights, the weighted objective J(h)=∑ w_k C_k is bilinear. We can solve the 2D pair subproblem exactly: For pair (i,j), J(t)=A t² + B t + const for active lags. Since t constrained interval. We can minimize over t exactly (quadratic). This avoids line search fractions and can find best pair move. Let's derive? For a pair move h_i += t, h_j -= t. For each active lag k, term C_k = ∑ h_a(1-h_b). The quadratic terms only where either a/b equals i/j. Contribution as function of t is at most quadratic if i and j appear in same product (like h_i(1-h_j)), else linear. We can compute coefficients for active lags: For each lag and pair, coefficients. But doing per candidate costly. However for pair (i,j), only lags k = i-j, j-i, 0? Wait products h_a(1-h_b) involve index pairs separated by s. If we change h_i and h_j, a product changes if one of its indices is i or j. That happens for lags s = i-j, j-i, and s such that pair? For h_i first with second i-s = j -> s=i-j. For h_i second with first i+s=j -> s=j-i. For h_j similarly. So only two lags! That's key: A single pair mass transfer only affects correlation values for lag equal to the distance between the two coordinates, plus maybe lag 0 if i=j (not), because products are only between coordinates at a fixed shift. Wait each h_i appears with h_{i-s} for each s. Changing h_i affects all lags s where the other index varies (n possibilities). So not only pair lag: h_i changes products with all h_{i-s} for all s, and h_j with all. Thus pair affects all lags in two rows: for h_i as first/second, for all s such that i-s valid; and for h_j. That's O(n) lags, not just 2. So exact J(t) quadratic if a product includes both i and j (lag i-j or j-i), linear otherwise. Sum over active lags: many linear. Could compute coefficients O(n_active?) but maybe not needed.

Let's detail `fix_sum` to guarantee. If all h are 0/1 and target integer, residual 0. If fractional and residual tiny, adjust one interior. If no interior, all values at bounds but sum mismatch (shouldn't). We can spread.
```
def fix_sum(h, target):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    for _ in range(20):
        res = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(res) <= 1e-14:
            break
        if res > 0:
            idx = np.where(h < 1.0)[0]
            if idx.size == 0: break
            order = idx[np.argsort(h[idx])[:min(idx.size, max(1, int(abs(res)*4)+4))]]
        else:
            idx = np.where(h > 0.0)[0]
            if idx.size == 0: break
            order = idx[np.argsort(-h[idx])[:min(idx.size, max(1, int(abs(res)*4)+4))]]
        rem = res
        for i in order:
            if abs(rem) <= 1e-14: break
            if rem > 0:
                add = min(rem, 1.0 - h[i])
                h[i] += add
                rem -= add
            else:
                sub = min(-rem, h[i])
                h[i] -= sub
                rem += sub
    # final if not
    res = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(res) > 1e-13:
        if res > 0:
            # distribute to all zeros/lows in chunks
            order = np.argsort(h)
        else:
            order = np.argsort(-h)
        rem=res
        for i in order:
            if abs(rem)<=1e-14: break
            if rem>0:
                add=min(rem,1-h[i]); h[i]+=add; rem-=add
            else:
                sub=min(-rem,h[i]); h[i]-=sub; rem+=sub
    return h
```
`project_density` can use bisection then fix_sum.

```
def binary_hill(h_float, deadline, max_iter=20000, temp=0.002):
  b=to_binary(h_float)
  n=b.size; cur_peak=peak_bin(b)
  best_b=b.copy(); best_peak=cur_peak
  cur_c=2*cur_peak/n
  rng...
  for it in range(max_iter):
    if time>deadline: break
    # get active gradient (using cur_peak corr maybe keep last corr)
    corr=bin_corr(b)
    peak=corr.max(); # If peak changed, update cur_c
    # decide move
    # 60% gradient pair, 40% random
    if rng.random()<0.7:
      top_count = max(8, int(0.03*n))
      g = active_gradient(b.astype(float), corr, top_count, 8)
      inc = np.where(b==0)[0]; choose inc_pool = k smallest g among inc (e.g. top 8)
      dec = np.where(b==1)[0]; dec_pool = k largest g among dec
      if inc_pool and dec_pool:
         i=inc_pool[int(rng.integers(len))]; j=dec_pool[int(rng.integers(len))]
    else:
       zeros=np.where(b==0)[0]; ones=np.where(b==1)[0]
       i=zeros[rng...]; j=ones[rng...]
    # swap
    cand=b.copy(); cand[i],cand[j]=1,0
    peak_cand=bin_corr(cand).max()
    # accept
    if peak_cand < cur_peak or (peak_cand==cur_peak and rng.random()<0.2) or rng.random()<np.exp(-(2*(peak_cand-cur_peak)/n)/temp):
       b=cand; cur_peak=peak_cand
       if cur_peak < best_peak: best...
  return b,best_c
```

```
def active_polish(h, deadline, top_frac=0.05, alpha=4.0, max_iter=100000, pair_pool=5, base_step=0.08):
    h = np.asarray(h,float)
    n=h.size; target=0.5*n
    best=project_density(h,target); best_c=eval_c5(best)
    cur=best.copy(); cur_c=best_c
    rng=np.random.default_rng(987)
    stall=0; it=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.perf_counter() >= deadline: break
       if n<2: break
       corr, _ = eval_corr(cur) # returns corr,n
       # If current is not finite? 
       top_count=int(np.clip(n*top_frac, 8, 64))
       top_count=min(top_count, corr.size)
       g=active_gradient(cur, corr, top_count, alpha)
       improved=False
       # Direction moves
       d=-g; d-=np.mean(d); norm=np.linalg.norm(d)
       if norm>1e-14:
          d=d/norm
          # Try a few step sizes; start with base_step and recent step? Use tuple.
          for t in (base_step*0.25, base_step*0.5, base_step, base_step*2.0, 0.01,0.2):
             cand=project_density(cur + t*d, target)
             cc=eval_c5(cand)
             if cc < cur_c - 1e-13:
                cur=cand; cur_c=cc; improved=True; break
          if improved:
             if cur_c < best_c - 1e-14: best=cur.copy(); best_c=cur_c
             stall=0
             base_step = min(0.5, base_step*1.1)
             continue
       # Pair moves
       # Build pools using g (use current g even if no d)
       inc_pool=[]; dec_pool=[]
       # Instead of argsort full each time (O(n log n)) okay. But many iterations; use argpartition? We need top 5. Use np.argpartition.
       if n>0:
          # inc: smallest g, but only h<1. We can get candidate indices low; filter.
          m=max(20, pair_pool*4)
          low_idx=np.argpartition(g, m)[:m]
          for idx in low_idx:
             if cur[idx] < 1.0-1e-8:
                inc_pool.append(int(idx)); 
             if len(inc_pool)>=pair_pool: break
          # If not enough, take random zeros
          if len(inc_pool)<pair_pool:
             zeros=np.where(cur<1-1e-8)[0]
             if zeros.size:
                add=np.array_split? simply for idx in rng.choice(zeros,size=pair_pool,...): append unique
          hi_idx=np.argpartition(-g, m)[:m]
          for idx in hi_idx:
             if cur[idx] > 1e-8:
                dec_pool.append(int(idx))
             if len>=pair_pool: break
          if len(dec_pool)<pair_pool:
             ones=np.where(cur>1e-8)[0]
             ...
       # Evaluate pairs
       best_pair_c=cur_c
       best_pair_cand=None
       for i in inc_pool:
         for j in dec_pool:
           if i==j: continue
           cap=min(1.0-cur[i], cur[j])
           if cap<1e-10: continue
           for frac in (0.5,1.0,0.2,0.8):
              # optimize: if frac*cap maybe
              cand=cur.copy(); cand[i]+=frac*cap; cand[j]-=frac*cap
              # clip/fix
              if cand[i] < -1e-12 or cand[i]>1+1e-12... continue
              cand=np.clip(cand,0,1); cand=fix_sum(cand,target)
              cc=eval_c5(cand)
              if cc < best_pair_c - 1e-13:
                  best_pair_c=cc; best_pair_cand=cand
       if best_pair_cand is not None:
          cur=best_pair_cand; cur_c=best_pair_c; improved=True
          if cur_c<best_c: update
          stall=0
          base_step=min(0.5, base_step*1.05)
          continue
       # Random pair transfer (a few)
       if n>=2:
         for _ in range(10):
           i=int(rng.integers(n)); j=int(rng.integers(n))
           if i==j or abs(cur[i]-cur[j])<1e-12: continue
           # If want move from higher to lower? conditioned by g? Random
           cap=min(1.0-cur[i], cur[j])
           if cap<1e-8: continue
           frac=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.8))
           cand=...
           cc=...
           if cc<cur_c-1e-13: accept break
       if improved: restore else:
          stall +=1
          if stall == 1 or stall == 5 or stall == 10 or stall == 20 or stall % 50 ==0:
             sig=max(0.002, 0.005*min(1.0,stall/50.0))
             # perturb from best, not cur, to avoid drift
             cur=project_density(best + rng.normal(0, sig, n), target)
             cur_c=eval_c5(cur)
             if cur_c < best_c: update? (since cur maybe best + noise likely worse)
             base_step=max(0.02, base_step*0.5)
          if stall > 200:
             cur=best.copy(); cur_c=best_c; stall=0; base_step=0.05
             # maybe break if base too small? no, continue with random
    return project_density(best,target), eval_c5(best)
```

```
def binary_hill(h, deadline, max_iter=100000, top_frac=0.04, alpha=8.0, candidates=3, temp0=0.004, temp_end=0.0005):
  b=to_binary_rank(h)
  n=b.size; target=n//2
  corr=binary_corr(b)
  cur_peak=int(corr.max()); cur_c=2*cur_peak/n
  best_b=b.copy(); best_peak=cur_peak; best_c=cur_c
  if n<2: return ...
  rng=default_rng(555)
  temp=temp0
  it=0
  total=max_iter
  for it in range(total):
    if time>=deadline: break
    if total>100: temp=temp0 + (temp_end-temp0)*min(1,it/(total//2))
    # If cur_peak maybe not update? keep
    # Gradient
    hfloat=b.astype(np.float64) # maybe cache? after swap only two values; but active_gradient uses h each pos for all lags; recompute float cast cost O(n), okay.
    g=active_gradient(hfloat, corr.astype(np.float64), max(8,int(n*top_frac)), alpha)
    # generate candidates set to avoid duplicates
    cand_list=[]
    zeros=np.nonzero(b==0)[0]; ones=np.nonzero(b==1)[0]
    # active gradient pools
    m=max(10, candidates*6)
    inc_pool = np.argpartition(g, m)[:m] filtered b==0
    dec_pool = np.argpartition(-g, m)[:m] filtered b==1
    # Score combos and pick candidates
    combos=[]
    for i in inc_pool:
      for j in dec_pool:
        if b[i]==0 and b[j]==1 and i!=j:
           combos.append((g[j]-g[i], i,j))
    # random combos
    for _ in range(candidates*2):
       i=zeros[rng.integers(zeros.size)]; j=ones[rng.integers(ones.size)]
       combos.append((rng.random(),i,j))
    combos.sort(reverse=True) maybe by first element.
    # evaluate up to `candidates` unique
    best_cand=None; best_cand_peak=cur_peak; best_score=None
    seen=set()
    for score,i,j in combos[:candidates*candidates?]:
       if (i,j) in seen: continue
       seen.add((i,j))
       cand=b.copy(); cand[i]=1; cand[j]=0
       ccorr=binary_corr(cand); peak=int(ccorr.max())
       c=2*peak/n
       # score for minimization: peak, maybe second max sum top? compute top2? Could use ccorr.sum? no.
       if peak < best_cand_peak or (peak==best_cand_peak and (best_cand is None or int(ccorr.sum()) < best_cand_sum?)):
           best_cand=cand; best_cand_peak=peak; best_cand_corr=ccorr; break? We need accept based on temp for maybe worse? If best cand not better, still maybe accept worse. Choose first cand minimal peak; if it is worse than cur, evaluate accept prob.
```

Potential speed improvement: For binary_hill, update corr incrementally O(n) instead of full O(n²). This would allow many more iterations and is worthwhile. Let's derive an O(n) update for full correlation array F(k) under swapping a 0 to1 at i and 1 to0 at j.

We maintain `corr` array length 2n-1, with index r=s+offset, shift s: F(s)=∑_{valid} h[first]*(1-h[second]), where first index = second+s? Earlier: for s positive: F(s)=∑_{q=0}^{n-1-s} h[q+s]*(1-h[q]). For s negative: s=-l: F(-l)=∑_{q=0}^{n-1-l} h[q]*(1-h[q+l]). Let's denote a pair contribution for ordered pair (u,v) where u is first h in product, v is second in (1-h): contribution = h[u]*(1-h[v]). The shift s = u - v. Since u = v+s. In F(s), sum over v where u=v+s valid. So each ordered pair (v,u) with difference s contributes a product. Good.

When swapping i (0->1, δ=+1) and j (1->0, δ=-1), contributions for any pair where u=i or v=i or u=j or v=j change. We can compute delta for each s efficiently using slices.

For a coordinate p with change δ_p (+1 or -1), its effect on all F(s):
- As first index u=p: pairs (v=p-s), s = p-v. For s such that v=p-s valid [0,n-1]. Contribution old = old_hp*(1-h[p-s]). New = (old_hp+δ)*(1-h[v]) if δ applied to hp. Delta = δ*(1-h[p-s]). This affects F(s) for s = p-v, v valid => s∈[-p, n-1-p]. δ positive for i, negative for j.
- As second index v=p: pairs (u=p+s), s = u-p. Contribution old = h[u]*(1-old_hp). New = h[u]*(1-old_hp - δ) if hp changes. Delta = -δ*h[u]. Affects F(s) for s∈[p-(n-1), p]. (u=p+s valid).
Sum for i and j. So total delta over s can be vectorized through intervals.

We need update corr array for candidate. We can compute a zero-length array length 2n-1 and add contributions. But doing vectorized slices for four terms per swap O(n) and then max O(n). This is O(n) with Python/numpy overhead but less than O(n²). For n=900, O(900) vs 810k, huge.

Let's implement `predict_correlation_after_swap(corr, h, i, j)` for binary? We can do generic h values 0/1 and swap i (0 to1), j (1 to0). Let old hi=0, hj=1. δ_i=+1, δ_j=-1.
For each changed p, δ:
1. first: F[s] += δ*(1 - h[p-s]) for valid p-s.
2. second: F[s] += -δ*h[p+s] for valid p+s.

Thus update for generic swap:
```
def update_corr_after_swap_c(corr, h, i, j, off):
 # assume h[i]=0,h[j]=1 and after swap changing i +1, j -1
 d = corr.astype(float?) maybe int? For binary counts, deltas can be -1,0,1; but if h continuous, deltas values. For binary h 0/1, δ_i=1, δ_j=-1.
 h = h float/int
 h_rev = h[::-1]
 hi_1 = 1.0 - h_rev  # length n
 # contributions for p=i, delta=+1
 add = hi_1 * +1
 corr_slice_first = corr[off+i-n+1 : off+i+1]; add to 
 add2 = -1 * h # for second term p=i δ=+1
 corr[off-i : off-i+n] += add2
 # p=j, delta=-1
 add = -1 * hi_1
 corr[off+j-n+1 : off+j+1] += add
 add2 = -(-1)*h = +h
 corr[off-j : off-j+n] += add
 return corr
```
But wait: If we apply both p=i and p=j to the **old** h, are we double-counting the pair where both i and j are involved? The contribution delta for a term h[u]*(1-h[v]) if both u and v change should include cross term δ_u δ_v? Our linear delta formula assumes other variable unchanged. If both coordinates change, a term where u=i and v=j changes from old 0*(1-1)=0 to new 1*(1-0)=1. Linear contributions: first p=i δ=+1: δ*(1-h[j]old=0)=0. second p=j δ=-1: -δ*h[i]old = +1*0=0. Total 0, missing +1. Similarly term u=j,v=i old 1*(1-0)=1 to new 0*(1-1)=0: linear: p=j first δ=-1*(1-h[i]old=1) = -1; p=i second -(+1)*h[j]old = -1; total -2, but actual change -1. So linear misses cross terms and even direction wrong? Let's compute carefully using product h_u(1-h_v). If both change: new - old = (h_u+δ_u)(1-h_v-δ_v) - h_u(1-h_v) = δ_u(1-h_v) - h_u δ_v - δ_u δ_v. The linear terms omit -δ_uδ_v. For binary swap, δ_i=+1, δ_j=-1, δ_iδ_j=-1, so extra +1 for term u=i,v=j; and +1? For term u=j,v=i: δ_jδ_i=-1 extra +1? Need add cross correction for pairs where (u,v) = (i,j) and (j,i). In F, term u=i,v=j exists with shift s=i-j; term u=j,v=i exists with shift s=j-i. Correction for term (u,v) = δ_u δ_v? From formula extra = -δ_uδ_v = -(-1)=+1 for both. Wait for both terms, δ_uδ_v = -1, extra +1. So add +1 to C[off + i - j] and +1 to C[off + j - i]. Important.

Thus we can implement exact O(n) update for binary swaps. This is valuable. Could also update peak with max after update O(n). No need `np.correlate` for each candidate!
The update uses slices with full length n arrays `h` and `1-h_rev`; O(n) memory copies per slice. For n=900, 4 slices length900 + set two points + max length1799. Very fast, maybe 0.00005s. Can do millions.

Let's implement `binary_corr_initial(b)` using np.correlate O(n²) once. Then `binary_swap_update(corr, b, i, j)` returns new corr (modified copy) and new b? We can update in candidate `corr_c = corr.copy()` and `b_c = b.copy()`. The slice additions use b old values. Since values int16, deltas int? The linear additions are 0/±1. Use int16 arrays; cross/self adjustments. Let's implement with float? Counts up to n, deltas ±2. int16 safe. But slice add arrays `h` int16 might promote? Use int16. However `1 - h_view` with h int16 result int16. Slices add. Good.

```
def binary_swap_update(corr, b, i, j):
    # corr length 2n-1 int16, b int16 0/1, assumes b[i]==0,b[j]==1
    n=b.size; off=n-1
    # copy corr and b
    nc = corr.copy()
    hb = b.astype(np.int16) maybe already
    h_rev = hb[::-1]
    one_minus_hrev = (1 - h_rev).astype(np.int16)
    # p=i, delta=+1
    nc[off+i-n+1 : off+i+1] += one_minus_hrev  # length n
    nc[off-i : off-i+n] -= hb                    # -delta*hb
    # p=j, delta=-1
    nc[off+j-n+1 : off+j+1] -= one_minus_hrev
    nc[off+j : off+j+n] += hb
    # self correction -2 at off
    nc[off] -= 2
    # cross corrections
    r1 = off + i - j
    r2 = off + j - i
    # They should be within 0..2n-2. For safety check
    if 0 <= r1 < nc.size: nc[r1] += 1
    if 0 <= r2 < nc.size: nc[r2] += 1
    # update b
    b2 = b.copy()
    b2[i] = 1
    b2[j] = 0
    return b2, nc
```
Let's test with continuous? We only use binary. Verify self correction: always -2? For binary δ_i=+1,δ_j=-1. If one of i/j? distinct. Yes. But what if h[i]=0, h[j]=1 exactly; self linear sum = δ_i(1-h_i)-h_iδ_i + δ_j(1-h_j)-h_jδ_j =1*1 -0 + (-1)*0 -1*(-? let's compute) =1+1=2. Actual self changes 0. So -2. Good.
Cross correction: For each order (u=i,v=j) and (u=j,v=i), extra -δ_uδ_v = +1. There are two terms if i≠j. Add +1 each. Good.

This is a major improvement! We can implement **exact pair transfer search** continuous in O(n log?) per candidate. Then local search can aggressively optimize.

For a pair (i,j), with i to increase, j decrease, a∈[0,cap] where cap=min(1-h_i,h_j). New corr C(a) = C0 + a L + a² E, where L is an integer/float array length 2n-1, 0 for most lags: for each p, δ sign:
- p=i, δ=+1: first slice L[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] += 1 - h_rev; second L[off-i:off-i+n] -= h.
- p=j, δ=-1: first L[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] -= (1 - h_rev); second L[off+j:off+j+n] += h.
For a, linear add a*L.
Extra E: -2 at off, +1 at r1, +1 at r2 (for a²). For binary a=1. For continuous, yes.
Let's test if h values arbitrary: For linear first/second using old h, extra as above. Good.

C(a) = max_l (C_l + a L_l + a² E_l). E_l is 0 except three positions with -2 or +1. If a² coefficient can increase at one lag due cross +a². The max of quadratics/linear over interval. This pair transfer search can be the core local optimizer. We can use gradient to propose pairs; then exact evaluate and choose best a by scanning. This avoids full `np.correlate` except to refresh corr after accepting a move (or maintain corr via update). We can maintain corr exactly! After accepting move, we can set corr = new_corr (computed) and h = new_h (just two values changed). Then no need full correlate every iteration. This is huge. Need ensure corr remains exact; formula exact, floating errors small. We can periodically recompute full correlate to clean drift (every 100 accepted moves) and update max.

Let's design a **pair_transfer polishing** algorithm:
State: h (float), corr (float array full), current C = max(corr)*dx. But for local search, we can compare using corr max (dx constant). Use float target.
At each iteration:
- Current max_m = corr.max() (or track). C = 2*max*m/n.
- Compute active lags top from corr.
- Compute gradient g via active_gradient (or directly from active lags) to score pairs.
- Generate candidate pairs (i,j) where i will increase (h_i<1), j decrease (h_j>0). Score based on gradient: We want change direction that reduces active lag. The derivative of weighted active objective at a=0 is a sum over active lags: For pair transfer, derivative dC_a? We can compute using L: derivative at 0 for lag l = L_l. For a weighted objective ∑w_k C_k, slope = ∑ w_k L_k. We can compute slope for a candidate pair cheaply once L computed. But for scoring many pairs before L, use gradient: The continuous gradient g (sum weights * derivative wrt h) tells if increasing i and decreasing j reduces objective: derivative along transfer a (i +a,j -a) = a*(g_i - g_j) (since objective linear approx). We want g_i - g_j <0 (decrease). So score = g_j - g_i (positive good). We'll use top low g inc, high g dec.
- For top pairs, compute L (and E points) once O(n). Then find best a in [0,cap] by scanning (e.g. 16 points) evaluating max of corr + a*L + a²E. Choose a giving lowest max. If new_max < current_max - tol, accept: update h[i]+=a,h[j]-=a, corr = new_corr, update current_max. Also update if recurring.
- If no improvement, maybe try random pairs or reverse sign (swap inc/dec). If stall, perturb h and recompute corr? Perturbation changes many h; need recompute full corr. Do occasionally.

This is far more efficient and can run millions of pair evaluations. It can also tune fractional moves continuously, likely beat target.

Let's verify exact update formula thoroughly.
Definitions: corr[r] = F(s=r-off) = ∑_{v: u=v+s valid} h_u * (1 - h_v). For a transfer: h_i += a, h_j -= a. Let δ_i=a, δ_j=-a, others 0.
For each ordered pair (u,v), change Δ_{uv} = (h_u+δ_u)(1 - h_v - δ_v) - h_u(1-h_v) = δ_u(1-h_v) - h_u δ_v - δ_uδ_v.
Sum over u,v for each s.
First term sum_u δ_u (1-h_v): for fixed u=p, contributes δ_p(1-h_v) to s=p-v. That's first slice with δ sign, values (1-h_v) reversed? Our L first slice for p with δ=+1: add (1-h[v]) to s=p-v, r=off+p-v. In increasing r (s increasing), v decreases, so h_rev. For δ=-1, subtract. Good.
Second term -∑_v h_u δ_v: for fixed v=p, -δ_p h_u to s=u-p, r=off+u-p; increasing u, h forward. Good.
Extra term -∑_{u,v both changed} δ_uδ_v. Changed set {i,j}. Ordered pairs: (i,i): -a² at s=0; (j,j): -a²; (i,j): -a(-a)=+a² at s=i-j; (j,i): +a² at s=j-i. Good. If i=j not allowed. If shifts coincide? self s=0 could coincide with i-j if i=j only; i≠j so distinct unless d=0. r1,r2 could equal off? no.
Thus formula exact.

We can implement `pair_linear_kernel(h, i, j, off)`:
```
def pair_linear_kernel(h, i, j):
    n=h.size; off=n-1; ncorr=2*n-1
    L = np.zeros(ncorr, dtype=float)
    h_rev = h[::-1]
    one_minus_hrev = 1.0 - h_rev
    # i delta +1
    L[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] += one_minus_hrev
    L[off-i:off-i+n] -= h
    # j delta -1
    L[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] -= one_minus_hrev
    L[off+j:off+j+n] += h
    return L, off, r1=off+i-j, r2=off+j-i
```
But for j, second term +h because -δ_p h with δ=-1. Yes.

Then candidate corr for a:
```
new = corr + a*L
new[off] -= 2*a*a
new[r1] += a*a
new[r2] += a*a
```
Need ensure r1,r2 valid; they are because i-j in [-(n-1),n-1]. If r1==r2? only i=j; no. If r1 or r2 equals off? only if d=0; no. Good.

When h contains floats, L values between -1 and 1 etc; a up to cap; new correlation should satisfy sum? The relation? okay.

We can use this for full precision and no full evaluate. To accept, compare `new_max = float(np.max(new))`. Since dx constant, max. Objective lower if new_max lower. Tolerance maybe 1e-12.

We need initial corr: `np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full')`. For continuous h. After many updates, corr exact modulo floating. We can recompute periodically to avoid drift:
```
corr, _ = eval_corr(h) # refresh, and update max
```
But this O(n²); every 200 accepted moves okay.

Detailed `pair_transfer_polish`:
```
def pair_transfer_polish(h, deadline, top_frac=0.04, alpha=4.0, max_iter=1000000, pair_pool=8, n_a=7, recompute_every=300):
    h=project_density(h,0.5*n)
    corr, _ = eval_corr(h)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_max=float(np.max(corr)); best_c=2*best_max/n
    cur_useful? current_max=best_max
    stall=0; accepted=0; it=0
    rng=default_rng(7)
    last_pair=None
    # candidate a fractions list
    fracs = (0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.55,0.8,1.0) but maybe 8
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time>=deadline: break
       # maybe recompute if drift or after perturb
       if accepted % recompute_every ==0 and accepted>0:
          corr, _ = eval_corr(h)
          current_max=float(corr.max())
          # current h sum fix? 
       # compute current_max from corr if not updated
       current_max=float(np.max(corr))
       if 2*current_max/n < best_c: best_c=...; best_h=h.copy()
       # candidate pair list
       pairs=[]
       # gradient based
       top_count=int(np.clip(n*top_frac, 8, 64))
       g=active_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha)
       m=max(12, pair_pool*3)
       low_idx=np.argpartition(g, m)[:m]
       for idx in low_idx:
          if h[idx] < 1-1e-10:
             pairs.append((g[idx], int(idx), 'inc'))? We need pair.
```

We need evaluate candidate pair's L once and best a.
Function `evaluate_pair(h, corr, i, j, fracs)` returns new_max, new_corr (for best a), a. To avoid creating new_corr for every a, compute `maxv` and store best_a; after loops if best_max < current_max improve, then create new_corr for best_a. But we may need best_corr for acceptance.
```
def best_pair_move(h, corr, i, j, fracs, current_max, tol):
    n=h.size; off=n-1
    cap=min(1-h[i],h[j])
    if cap <=1e-12: return None
    L = np.zeros(2*n-1)
    h_rev=h[::-1]; om=1-h_rev
    L[off+i-n+1:off+i+1]+=om
    L[off-i:off-i+n]-=h
    L[off+j-n+1:off+j+1]-=om
    L[off+j:off+j+n]+=h
    r1=off+i-j; r2=off+j-i
    best_a=None; best_max=current_max; best_corr=None
    # To avoid evaluating a=0, fracs positive. Include maybe 1.0
    for frac in fracs:
       a=frac*cap
       if a<=1e-15: continue
       cand = corr + a*L  # creates array
       cand[off] -= 2*a*a
       cand[r1] += a*a
       cand[r2] += a*a
       # due floating, might have tiny negative? no
       mx=float(np.max(cand))
       if mx < best_max - 1e-14:
          best_max=mx; best_a=a; best_corr=cand
       # if we found mx slightly less but best_a... continue
    if best_corr is not None:
       # optionally refine around best_a? If best_a not at a frac, but we only have discrete. Could do local 3-point around best_frac? Maybe not.
       return best_a,best_max,best_corr
    else:
       # For annealing, maybe return anc with small max? We'll not.
       return None
```

Let's code active_gradient efficiently:
```
def active_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha):
    n=h.size; off=n-1; L=corr.size
    top_count=min(max(1,top_count),L)
    if top_count == L:
        idx=np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx=np.argpartition(corr, L-top_count)[-top_count:]
    vals=corr[idx]
    maxv=vals.max(); minv=vals.min(); rng=maxv-minv
    if rng < 1e-12:
       w=np.full(top_count,1.0/top_count)
    else:
       w=np.exp((vals-maxv)/rng * alpha)
       w=np.clip(w,1e-15,1.0)
       w/=w.sum()
    g=np.zeros(n)
    h_rev? no.
    for t,wt in zip(idx,w):
       if wt < 1e-12: continue
       s=int(t)-off
       if s==0:
          g += wt*(1.0-2.0*h)
       elif s>0:
          g[s:] += wt
          g[s:] -= wt*h[:n-s]
          g[:n-s] -= wt*h[s:]
       else:
          l=-s
          g[:n-l] += wt
          g[:n-l] -= wt*h[l:]
          g[l:] -= wt*h[:n-l]
    return g
```
Check for s>0: first term derivative from h[i] as first with i=s..n-1: 1 - h[i-s]. We add 1 to g[s:]; subtract h[:n-s] from g[s:]. second term for h[i+s] as second with i=0..n-1-s: -h[i+s]; we subtract h[s:] from g[:n-s]. Correct.

For s<0, l=-s: F(-l)=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-l} h[i]*(1-h[i+l]). Derivative: first i=0..n-1-l: 1-h[i+l] -> g[:n-l]+=wt; g[:n-l]-=wt*h[l:]; second i=l..n-1: -h[i-l] -> g[l:]-=wt*h[:n-l]. Correct.

Now, `pair_linear` slices orientation should align with this gradient. We tested. Good.

Max unaffected if negative tiny. But if many updates, drift. Periodic recompute. If negative tiny, objective max maybe lower slightly but final full eval correct. We should not clip corr in local search because objective exact; but tiny negative okay. If during pair update an entry becomes -1e-12, max maybe less, could accept a move that appears better due numerical drift. Use tolerance `1e-10` and periodic recompute. Fine.

Let's define `eval_c5(h)` for final:
```
def eval_c5(h):
   n=h.size
   if n==0: return inf
   corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, 'full')
   return float(np.max(corr) * (2.0/n))
```
`eval_corr(h)` returns corr,n.

```
import time
import numpy as np

def fix_sum(...): ...
```

Let's detail `project_density`:
```
def project_density(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).copy()
    if x.ndim !=1: x=x.ravel()
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.5, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.clip(x,0.0,1.0)
    n=x.size
    if n==0: return x
    target=float(target)
    s=float(x.sum())
    if abs(s-target) > 1e-12:
       lo,hi=-1.0,1.0
       for _ in range(70):
           mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
           s=float(np.sum(np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0)))
           if s < target: lo=mid
           else: hi=mid
       x=np.clip(x+0.5*(lo+hi),0.0,1.0)
    return fix_sum(x, target)
```
`fix_sum` as above.

`fix_sum` must handle target not exact. In loop, `int(abs(res)*4)` if res huge? But after bisection small. For initial all 0.5 target n/2 res 0. If res large from `make_ternary` maybe exact. Good.

`make_diverse_start`: ensure project.

`upsample_h`: if parent size n0, target n. x0 centers `(np.arange(n0)+0.5)*(2/n0)`, x1 similarly. Use `np.interp`. If parent values not sorted, okay.

`active_lags_and_gradient`:
```
def active_lags_and_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha):
    n=h.size; off=n-1; m=corr.size
    top_count=int(min(max(1, int(top_count)), m))
    if top_count >= m:
       idx=np.arange(m)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(corr, m-top_count)[-top_count:]
    vals=corr[idx]
    maxv=float(vals.max()); minv=float(vals.min()); rng=maxv-minv
    if rng < 1e-12:
       w=np.full(top_count, 1.0/top_count)
    else:
       w=np.exp(((vals=maxv?)/rng)*alpha) # careful values differences up to range; max weight 1, min exp(-alpha)
       # If alpha large, exp(0)=1, exp(-alpha). Good.
       w=np.clip(w, 1e-15, None)
       wsum=w.sum()
       if wsum <=0: uniform
       w /= wsum
    g=np.zeros(n,float)
    for t,wt in zip(idx,w):
       wt=float(wt)
       if wt < 1e-14: continue
       s=int(t)-off
       ...
    return g, idx
```
If corr contains negative tiny, maxv maybe; fine.

```
def pair_transfer_polish(h, deadline, top_frac=0.05, alpha_base=5.0, max_iter=10000000, pair_pool=6, max_candidates=24, recompute_every=400, anneal_start_c=1e-4):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    n=h.size; target=0.5*n
    if n<2: return h, eval_c5(h)
    h=project_density(h,target)
    corr,_=eval_corr(h)
    current_max=float(corr.max())
    current_c=2.0*current_max/n
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=current_c
    rng=np.random.default_rng(1)
    fracs=(0.003,0.01,0.03,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.8,1.0)
    ncorr=2*n-1; off=n-1
    L=np.empty(ncorr); tmp=np.empty(ncorr)
    stall=0; accepted=0; it=0
    alpha=alpha_base
    # maybe alpha_cycle
    alphas=(2.0,5.0,10.0,20.0)
    alpha_idx=1
    anneal_tol_c=anneal_start_c
    recompute_counter=0
    while it < max_iter:
       if time.perf_counter() >= deadline: break
       it +=1
       # current_max from corr
       # If corr has drifted from h? periodic recompute below after accepted.
       if 2.0*current_max/n < best_c - 1e-15:
           best_c=2.0*current_max/n; best_h=h.copy()
       # top_count
       top_count=int(np.clip(0.06*n if n<200 else 0.05*n, 8, 64)) # maybe
       g, idx = active_lags_and_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha)
       h_rev=h[::-1]
       om=1.0 - h_rev
       candidates=[]
       seen=set()
       def add? no closures. Use inline repeated.
```

Candidate evaluation:
```
strict_i=-1; strict_j=-1; strict_a=0.0; strict_max=current_max
any_i=-1; any_max=float('inf'); any_a=0.0
for score,i,j in cands:
    cap = min(1.0 - h[i], h[j])
    if cap <= 1e-12: continue
    L.fill(0.0)
    L[off+i-n+1:off+i+1] += om
    L[off-i:off-i+n] -= h
    L[off+j-n+1:off+j+1] -= om
    L[off+j:off+j+n] += h
    r1=off+i-j; r2=off+j-i
    pair_best_max = current_max # for strict improvement < current
    pair_best_a=0.0
    # track any best with value maybe infinite
    any_pair_max = float('inf')
    any_pair_a=0.0
    for frac in fracs:
       a=frac*cap
       if a <=1e-14: continue
       np.multiply(L, a, out=tmp)
       np.add(corr, tmp, out=tmp)
       aa=a*a
       tmp[off] -= 2.0*aa
       tmp[r1] += aa
       tmp[r2] += aa
       mx=float(tmp.max())
       if mx < pair_best_max - 1e-14:
           pair_best_max=mx; pair_best_a=a
       if mx < any_pair_max:
           any_pair_max=mx; any_pair_a=a
    if pair_best_a > 0 and pair_best_max < strict_max - 1e-14:
       strict_max=pair_best_max; strict_i=i; strict_j=j; strict_a=pair_best_a
    if any_pair_a > 0 and any_pair_max < any_max:
       any_max=any_pair_max; any_i=i; any_j=j; any_a=any_pair_a
```
`pair_best_max` initialized current_max; if a slightly worsens but still lowest among any, any tracks. Good.

```
def add_candidate(cands, seen, score, i, j, h, n):
    if i == j: return
    if i < 0 or i >= n or j <0 or j>=n: return
    if h[i] >= 1.0 - 1e-10 or h[j] <= 1e-10: return
    key = i * n + j
    if key in seen: return
    seen.add(key)
    cands.append((float(score), i, j))
```

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.perf_counter()
    budget = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 1300.0
    deadline = start + max(10.0, budget - 20.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        raw = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if raw.ndim == 1 and raw.size >= 2:
            parent = np.nan_to_num(raw, nan=0.5, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
            parent = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1.0).copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size >= 2:
        n0 = parent.size
        best_h = project_density(parent, 0.5*n0)
        best_c = eval_c5(best_h)
        best_n = n0
    else:
        n0 = 450
        x=(np.arange(n0)+0.5)*2.0/n0
        best_h = 0.5 + 0.3*np.sin(2*np.pi*3*x/2) + 0.15*np.sin(2*np.pi*5*x/2+1.0)
        best_h = project_density(best_h,0.5*n0)
        best_c=eval_c5(best_h); best_n=n0
```

`try_add_candidate` top-level:
```
def try_add_candidate(cands, seen, score, i, j, h, n):
    if i == j or i < 0 or i >= n or j < 0 or j >= n:
        return
    if h[i] >= 1.0 - 1e-10 or h[j] <= 1e-10:
        return
    key = i * n + j
    if key in seen:
        return
    seen.add(key)
    cands.append((float(score), int(i), int(j)))
```
Score could be numpy float; convert.

Now, let's think about if the parent `initial_h_values` is a Python list, `np.asarray` works. If it is pre-imported but None? try catches? if None np.asarray(None) gives array(nan?) maybe ndim 0; fails. Good.

Now, final code length maybe ~500 lines? okay.

Let's now compose final code carefully. Ensure no tabs? okay.

One more idea: For final high precision, we can call pair_transfer_polish with `recompute_every` smaller and fracs include tiny `0.001`. The default fracs 0.003,0.01... If cap=1, a=0.003. For M reduce 0.001, enough. If need smaller, repeated moves. Include 0.001? Let's set fracs in function parameter `fracs` default `(0.002, 0.005, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0)` (9). Slight overhead. Use in calls? We can allow parameter.

Define `pair_transfer_polish(..., fracs=None)` and if None default. In run final, pass smaller? Use same.

In candidate eval, `for frac in fracs:` if `frac*cap` maybe duplicates if cap small; okay.

Time: 9 fracs * candidates. Fine.

Let's write final now. Ensure all indentation correct.

Potential issue: `np.argpartition(corr, m - top_count)[-top_count:]` when `m-top_count` equals m? top_count=0 not. Good.

Potential issue: In `active_lags_and_gradient`, if `top_count` > m set m. For n=2, top_count maybe 8 but m=3, top_count=3. Good.

Potential issue: In `pair_transfer_polish`, `m_pool = min(n - 1, max(12, 3 * pair_pool))`. If n=2, m_pool=1? Use `min(n, ...)`. `argpartition` kth must < n; if m_pool==n, use arange. So:
```
if m_pool >= n:
   low = np.arange(n)
else: low = np.argpartition(g, m_pool)[:m_pool]
```
Good.

Direct active diff top k: if diff.size ==0 skip. `k_pick = min(3, diff.size)`. If k_pick >= diff.size: vidx=np.arange(diff.size) else argpartition(diff, diff.size-k_pick)[-k_pick:].

In random pairs, if n large and all zeros/ones? random may pick infeasible; loop 8.

Let's ensure `try_add_candidate` uses score `float(score)`, if score is np.nan, float nan; sorting with nan? `np.isfinite`? Candidate scores from h differences finite; rng finite. No issue.

Now, in `run`, after phase1, `best_h` from pair_transfer is projected. Good.

Variant loop: Use `variants=[]` top-level local. Add if size same as best. If variant c initial already lower, update before polish. We can evaluate and update.
```
	for v in variants:
        cv=eval_c5(v)
        if cv < best_c: best_h=v; best_c=cv; best_n=v.size
        rem=...
```
Good.

High res: after upsample, evaluate and update. Then polish. If polished lower update. If high-res initial c worse, still polish. Good.

Maybe if high-res size > best size, but C lower, update best_n. If high-res not lower, keep lower n best. Final polish best at its n.

Should we try downsampling high-res best to lower n? Maybe if high-res found structure, lower n might have lower C due coarser? Could downsample by block average? But return n free; high-res better expression. If high-res lower, keep. If not, no.

Potential issue: If best_h size changes high-res, variant loop before high-res used old size. okay.

Time reserve: final pair_transfer uses deadline exactly; after it returns, we do project/eval which takes some time. We set run deadline = start+budget-20, so reserve 20s for final eval. Good.

Now, one more mathematical check: The objective `eval_c5` uses `max(corr) * dx`, where corr = correlate(h,1-h). Our pair_transfer compares `max(corr)` (counts), since dx constant for fixed n. Across different n, dx different; final C = 2*max/n. We compare C across n in run. In pair_transfer at fixed n, comparing max counts equivalent. Good.

Now, if in run high-res c2 is lower than best_c, update. But high-res c2 computed by pair_transfer return (full eval). Good.

Let's think about **constraining sum exactly** in `pair_transfer`: The transfer preserves sum but due floating periodic fix. If we fix after recompute, the corr updated by pair moves between fixes is exact for h before fix; fix changes h tiny and recompute. Good.

Potential bug in `fix_sum`: The loop adjusts indices in order; if res positive and idx empty break. At the end, if abs(res)>1e-13, it does another full pass. But if there is no capacity (all h=1 and res<0 etc) impossible if target n/2 and h sum within? If all 0 and target positive, capacity. Good.

Now, final code. Let's ensure no `scipy` import, allowed. Use only numpy/time.

Potential issue: The harness may not allow `np.random.default_rng`? Numpy version likely. Good.

Let's final.
</think>

```python
import time
import numpy as np


def fix_sum(h, target):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = float(target)

    for _ in range(25):
        res = target - float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(res) <= 1e-14:
            break
        if res > 0.0:
            idx = np.where(h < 1.0)[0]
            if idx.size == 0:
                break
            cnt = min(idx.size, max(1, int(abs(res) * 4) + 4))
            idx = idx[np.argsort(h[idx])[:cnt]]
        else:
            idx = np.where(h > 0.0)[0]
            if idx.size == 0:
                break
            cnt = min(idx.size, max(1, int(abs(res) * 4) + 4))
            idx = idx[np.argsort(-h[idx])[:cnt]]

        rem = res
        for i in idx:
            if abs(rem) <= 1e-14:
                break
            if rem > 0.0:
                add = min(rem, 1.0 - h[i])
                h[i] += add
                rem -= add
            else:
                sub = min(-rem, h[i])
                h[i] -= sub
                rem += sub

    res = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(res) > 1e-13:
        order = np.argsort(h) if res > 0.0 else np.argsort(-h)
        rem = res
        for i in order:
            if abs(rem) <= 1e-14:
                break
            if rem > 0.0:
                add = min(rem, 1.0 - h[i])
                if add <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[i] += add
                rem -= add
            else:
                sub = min(-rem, h[i])
                if sub <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[i] -= sub
                rem += sub
    return h


def project_density(x, target):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float).copy()
    if x.ndim != 1:
        x = x.ravel()
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.5, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return x
    target = float(target)

    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if abs(s - target) > 1e-12:
        lo, hi = -1.0, 1.0
        for _ in range(70):
            mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
            s = float(np.sum(np.clip(x + mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
            if s < target:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid
        x = np.clip(x + 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    return fix_sum(x, target)


def upsample_h(parent, n):
    p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=float)
    n0 = p.size
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return p.copy()
    if n0 == 0:
        return fix_sum(np.full(n, 0.5), 0.5 * n)
    if n0 == n:
        return fix_sum(p, 0.5 * n)

    x0 = (np.arange(n0) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n0))
    x1 = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    h = np.interp(x1, x0, p, left=float(p[0]), right=float(p[-1]))
    return fix_sum(h, 0.5 * n)


def eval_corr(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = h.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=float), 0
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return corr, n


def eval_c5(h):
    corr, n = eval_corr(h)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(np.max(corr) * (2.0 / float(n)))


def make_diverse_start(n, rng, mode):
    n = int(n)
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n))
    if mode == 0:
        h = 0.5
        h += 0.32 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * 3.0 * x / 2.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 6.283)))
        h += 0.15 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * 5.0 * x / 2.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 6.283)))
    elif mode == 1:
        h = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
        seg = max(1, int(n // 8))
        for i in range(0, n, seg):
            e = min(i + seg, n)
            h[i:e] = float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.92))
    elif mode == 2:
        u = rng.random(n)
        med = float(np.median(u))
        h = np.where(u > med, 0.72, 0.28).astype(float)
        h += rng.normal(0.0, 0.04, n)
    else:
        f = int(rng.integers(2, 8))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 6.283))
        h = 0.5 + 0.38 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * x / 2.0 + ph)
        h += 0.12 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f + 3) * x / 2.0 + float(rng.uniform(0.0, 6.283)))
    return project_density(h, 0.5 * n)


def to_binary_rank(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = h.size
    out = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if n >= 2:
        idx = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
        out[idx[:n // 2]] = 1.0
    return project_density(out, 0.5 * n)


def make_ternary(h, k):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = h.size
    k = int(k)
    k = max(0, min(n // 2, k))
    out = np.full(n, 0.5, dtype=float)
    if k > 0:
        idx = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
        out[idx[:k]] = 1.0
        if k > 0:
            out[idx[-k:]] = 0.0
    return project_density(out, 0.5 * n)


def try_add_candidate(cands, seen, score, i, j, h, n):
    if i == j:
        return
    i = int(i)
    j = int(j)
    if i < 0 or i >= n or j < 0 or j >= n:
        return
    if h[i] >= 1.0 - 1e-10:
        return
    if h[j] <= 1e-10:
        return
    key = i * n + j
    if key in seen:
        return
    seen.add(key)
    cands.append((float(score), i, j))


def active_lags_and_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha):
    n = h.size
    off = n - 1
    m = corr.size
    top_count = int(min(max(1, int(top_count)), m))

    if top_count >= m:
        idx = np.arange(m)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(corr, m - top_count)[-top_count:]

    vals = corr[idx]
    maxv = float(np.max(vals)) if vals.size else 0.0
    minv = float(np.min(vals)) if vals.size else 0.0
    rngv = maxv - minv
    if rngv < 1e-12:
        w = np.full(top_count, 1.0 / float(top_count), dtype=float)
    else:
        w = np.exp(((vals - maxv) / rngv) * float(alpha))
        w = np.clip(w, 1e-15, None)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if ws <= 1e-30:
            w = np.full(top_count, 1.0 / float(top_count), dtype=float)
        else:
            w /= ws

    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for t, wt in zip(idx, w):
        wt = float(wt)
        if wt < 1e-14:
            continue
        s = int(t) - off
        if s == 0:
            g += wt * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
        elif s > 0:
            g[s:] += wt
            g[s:] -= wt * h[:n - s]
            g[:n - s] -= wt * h[s:]
        else:
            l = -s
            g[:n - l] += wt
            g[:n - l] -= wt * h[l:]
            g[l:] -= wt * h[:n - l]
    return g, idx


def pair_transfer_polish(h, deadline,
                         top_frac=0.05,
                         alpha_base=5.0,
                         max_iter=10**9,
                         pair_pool=6,
                         max_candidates=26,
                         recompute_every=450,
                         default_fracs=(0.002, 0.005, 0.01, 0.03, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.80, 1.00)):
    h = project_density(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.5 * int(h.size))
    n = int(h.size)
    if n < 2:
        return h, eval_c5(h)

    target = 0.5 * n
    corr, _ = eval_corr(h)
    current_max = float(np.max(corr))
    best_c = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
    best_h = h.copy()

    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)
    fracs = tuple(float(x) for x in default_fracs)
    ncorr = 2 * n - 1
    off = n - 1
    L = np.empty(ncorr, dtype=float)
    tmp = np.empty(ncorr, dtype=float)

    alphas = (2.0, 5.0, 10.0, 20.0)
    alpha_idx = 1
    alpha = float(min(max(alpha_base, 2.0), 20.0))
    anneal_start_c = 1e-4
    recompute_counter = 0
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    while it < int(max_iter):
        now = time.perf_counter()
        if now >= float(deadline):
            break
        it += 1

        cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
        if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c = cc
            best_h = h.copy()

        base_top = int(np.clip(max(1.0, float(top_frac) * n), 8.0, 64.0))
        top_count = min(96, base_top + (stall // 100) * 4)
        g, idx = active_lags_and_gradient(h, corr, top_count, alpha)

        h_rev = h[::-1]
        om = 1.0 - h_rev

        cands = []
        seen = set()

        pool_size = min(n, max(12, 3 * int(pair_pool)))
        if pool_size >= n:
            low_idx = np.arange(n)
        else:
            low_idx = np.argpartition(g, pool_size)[:pool_size]

        inc_pool = []
        for ii in low_idx:
            ii = int(ii)
            if h[ii] < 1.0 - 1e-10:
                inc_pool.append(ii)
            if len(inc_pool) >= int(pair_pool):
                break
        tries = 0
        while len(inc_pool) < int(pair_pool) and tries < int(pair_pool) * 8:
            tries += 1
            rr = int(rng.integers(n))
            if h[rr] < 1.0 - 1e-10 and rr not in inc_pool:
                inc_pool.append(rr)

        if pool_size >= n:
            high_idx = np.arange(n)
        else:
            high_idx = np.argpartition(-g, pool_size)[:pool_size]

        dec_pool = []
        for jj in high_idx:
            jj = int(jj)
            if h[jj] > 1e-10:
                dec_pool.append(jj)
            if len(dec_pool) >= int(pair_pool):
                break
        tries = 0
        while len(dec_pool) < int(pair_pool) and tries < int(pair_pool) * 8:
            tries += 1
            rr = int(rng.integers(n))
            if h[rr] > 1e-10 and rr not in dec_pool:
                dec_pool.append(rr)

        for i in inc_pool:
            for j in dec_pool:
                if i != j:
                    score = g[j] - g[i]
                    if score > -1e-10:
                        try_add_candidate(cands, seen, score, i, j, h, n)

        if idx.size:
            order = np.argsort(corr[idx])[::-1]
            for tt in idx[order[:6]]:
                s = int(tt) - off
                if s > 0:
                    if n - s > 0:
                        diff = h[s:] - h[:n - s]
                        if diff.size:
                            kp = min(3, diff.size)
                            if kp >= diff.size:
                                vidx = np.arange(diff.size)
                            else:
                                vidx = np.argpartition(diff, diff.size - kp)[-kp:]
                            for v in vidx:
                                v = int(v)
                                i = v + s
                                j = v
                                if h[j] > h[i] + 1e-10:
                                    try_add_candidate(cands, seen, float(h[j] - h[i]), i, j, h, n)
                elif s < 0:
                    l = -s
                    if n - l > 0:
                        diff = h[:n - l] - h[l:]
                        if diff.size:
                            kp = min(3, diff.size)
                            if kp >= diff.size:
                                vidx = np.arange(diff.size)
                            else:
                                vidx = np.argpartition(diff, diff.size - kp)[-kp:]
                            for v in vidx:
                                v = int(v)
                                i = v + l
                                j = v
                                if h[j] > h[i] + 1e-10:
                                    try_add_candidate(cands, seen, float(h[j] - h[i]), i, j, h, n)

        for _ in range(8):
            a = int(rng.integers(n))
            b = int(rng.integers(n))
            if a != b:
                if h[a] < 1.0 - 1e-10 and h[b] > 1e-10:
                    try_add_candidate(cands, seen, float(rng.random()), a, b, h, n)
                if h[b] < 1.0 - 1e-10 and h[a] > 1e-10:
                    try_add_candidate(cands, seen, float(rng.random()), b, a, h, n)

        if len(cands) > int(max_candidates):
            cands.sort(reverse=True)
            cands = cands[:int(max_candidates)]

        strict_i = -1
        strict_j = -1
        strict_a = 0.0
        strict_max = current_max

        any_i = -1
        any_j = -1
        any_a = 0.0
        any_max = float("inf")

        for _score, i, j in cands:
            cap = min(1.0 - h[i], h[j])
            if cap <= 1e-12:
                continue

            L.fill(0.0)
            L[i:i + n] += om
            L[n - 1 - i:n - 1 - i + n] -= h
            L[j:j + n] -= om
            L[n - 1 - j:n - 1 - j + n] += h

            r1 = off + i - j
            r2 = off + j - i

            pair_best_max = current_max
            pair_best_a = 0.0
            pair_any_max = float("inf")
            pair_any_a = 0.0

            for frac in fracs:
                a = float(frac) * cap
                if a <= 1e-14:
                    continue
                np.multiply(L, a, out=tmp)
                tmp += corr
                aa = a * a
                tmp[off] -= 2.0 * aa
                tmp[r1] += aa
                tmp[r2] += aa
                mx = float(np.max(tmp))
                if mx < pair_best_max - 1e-14:
                    pair_best_max = mx
                    pair_best_a = a
                if mx < pair_any_max:
                    pair_any_max = mx
                    pair_any_a = a

            if pair_best_a > 0.0 and pair_best_max < strict_max - 1e-14:
                strict_max = pair_best_max
                strict_i = i
                strict_j = j
                strict_a = pair_best_a

            if pair_any_a > 0.0 and pair_any_max < any_max:
                any_max = pair_any_max
                any_i = i
                any_j = j
                any_a = pair_any_a

        accepted = False
        if strict_i >= 0:
            i = int(strict_i)
            j = int(strict_j)
            a = float(strict_a)
            L.fill(0.0)
            L[i:i + n] += om
            L[n - 1 - i:n - 1 - i + n] -= h
            L[j:j + n] -= om
            L[n - 1 - j:n - 1 - j + n] += h
            r1 = off + i - j
            r2 = off + j - i
            np.multiply(L, a, out=tmp)
            tmp += corr
            aa = a * a
            tmp[off] -= 2.0 * aa
            tmp[r1] += aa
            tmp[r2] += aa

            corr = tmp.copy()
            h[i] += a
            h[j] -= a
            if h[i] > 1.0:
                h[i] = 1.0
            if h[j] < 0.0:
                h[j] = 0.0

            current_max = float(np.max(corr))
            cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
            if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cc
                best_h = h.copy()
            accepted = True
            stall = 0
            recompute_counter += 1

        else:
            anneal_tol_c = anneal_start_c * max(0.0, 1.0 - float(it) / 25000.0)
            anneal_tol = float(n) * 0.5 * anneal_tol_c
            if any_i >= 0 and any_a > 0.0 and any_max <= current_max + anneal_tol:
                prob = 0.10
                if stall > 50:
                    prob = 0.35
                if stall > 200:
                    prob = 0.60
                if rng.random() < prob:
                    i = int(any_i)
                    j = int(any_j)
                    a = float(any_a)
                    L.fill(0.0)
                    L[i:i + n] += om
                    L[n - 1 - i:n - 1 - i + n] -= h
                    L[j:j + n] -= om
                    L[n - 1 - j:n - 1 - j + n] += h
                    r1 = off + i - j
                    r2 = off + j - i
                    np.multiply(L, a, out=tmp)
                    tmp += corr
                    aa = a * a
                    tmp[off] -= 2.0 * aa
                    tmp[r1] += aa
                    tmp[r2] += aa

                    corr = tmp.copy()
                    h[i] += a
                    h[j] -= a
                    if h[i] > 1.0:
                        h[i] = 1.0
                    if h[j] < 0.0:
                        h[j] = 0.0
                    current_max = float(np.max(corr))
                    cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
                    if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                        best_c = cc
                        best_h = h.copy()
                    accepted = True
                    stall = 0
                    recompute_counter += 1

        if accepted:
            if recompute_counter >= int(recompute_every):
                h = fix_sum(h, target)
                corr, _ = eval_corr(h)
                current_max = float(np.max(corr))
                cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
                if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                    best_c = cc
                    best_h = h.copy()
                recompute_counter = 0
            continue

        stall += 1
        if stall in (25, 60, 100, 160):
            alpha_idx = (alpha_idx + 1) % len(alphas)
            alpha = float(alphas[alpha_idx])

        if stall % 120 == 0:
            sigma = 0.008 + 0.018 * min(1.0, float(stall) / 800.0)
            pert = best_h + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
            pert = project_density(pert, target)
            pc, _ = eval_corr(pert)
            pm = float(np.max(pc))
            h = pert
            corr = pc
            current_max = pm
            cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
            if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cc
                best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
            recompute_counter = 0

        if stall > 900:
            h = best_h.copy()
            corr, _ = eval_corr(h)
            current_max = float(np.max(corr))
            cc = 2.0 * current_max / float(n)
            if cc < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cc
            stall = 0
            alpha_idx = 0
            alpha = float(alphas[0])
            recompute_counter = 0

    best_h = fix_sum(best_h, target)
    best_c = eval_c5(best_h)
    return best_h, float(best_c)


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.perf_counter()
    budget = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 1300.0
    deadline = start + max(10.0, budget - 25.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        raw = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if raw.ndim == 1 and raw.size >= 2:
            parent = np.nan_to_num(raw, nan=0.5, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
            parent = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1.0).copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size >= 2:
        n0 = int(parent.size)
        best_h = project_density(parent, 0.5 * n0)
        best_c = eval_c5(best_h)
        best_n = n0
    else:
        n0 = 450
        x = (np.arange(n0) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(n0))
        init = 0.5
        init += 0.30 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * 3.0 * x / 2.0)
        init += 0.14 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * 5.0 * x / 2.0 + 1.0)
        best_h = project_density(init, 0.5 * n0)
        best_c = eval_c5(best_h)
        best_n = n0

    rem = deadline - time.perf_counter()
    if rem > 40.0:
        dl = time.perf_counter() + max(20.0, 0.42 * rem)
        h1, c1 = pair_transfer_polish(
            best_h, dl,
            top_frac=0.055,
            alpha_base=5.0,
            pair_pool=7,
            max_candidates=32,
            recompute_every=500
        )
        if c1 < best_c:
            best_h = h1
            best_c = c1
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

    variants = []
    nv = int(best_h.size)
    if nv >= 2:
        bvar = to_binary_rank(best_h)
        variants.append(bvar)
        variants.append(make_ternary(best_h, nv // 4))
        variants.append(make_ternary(best_h, nv // 2))
        variants.append(make_diverse_start(nv, rng, 0))

    for v in variants:
        rem = deadline - time.perf_counter()
        if rem < 50.0:
            break
        vc = eval_c5(v)
        if vc < best_c:
            best_h = v
            best_c = vc
            best_n = int(best_h.size)
        alloc = min(70.0, 0.10 * rem)
        dl = time.perf_counter() + max(20.0, alloc)
        hv, cv = pair_transfer_polish(
            v, dl,
            top_frac=0.065,
            alpha_base=8.0,
            pair_pool=5,
            max_candidates=20,
            recompute_every=350
        )
        if cv < best_c:
            best_h = hv
            best_c = cv
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

    possible_high_N = []
    base = int(best_h.size)
    for mult in (2, 4):
        tn = int(base * mult)
        if 2 <= tn <= 2400:
            possible_high_N.append(tn)
    for extra in (900, 1800, 2400):
        if extra > base and 2 <= extra <= 2400:
            possible_high_N.append(extra)

    for tn in sorted(set(possible_high_N)):
        rem = deadline - time.perf_counter()
        if rem < 220.0:
            break
        if int(tn) <= base:
            continue
        h_up = upsample_h(best_h, tn)
        c_up = eval_c5(h_up)
        if c_up < best_c:
            best_h = h_up
            best_c = c_up
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

        alloc = min(0.40 * rem, 700.0)
        dl = time.perf_counter() + max(40.0, alloc)
        top_frac = 0.045 if int(tn) <= 1000 else 0.035
        maxc = 24 if int(tn) <= 1000 else 16
        pool = 6 if int(tn) <= 1000 else 5
        hv, cv = pair_transfer_polish(
            h_up, dl,
            top_frac=top_frac,
            alpha_base=4.0,
            pair_pool=pool,
            max_candidates=maxc,
            recompute_every=700
        )
        if cv < best_c:
            best_h = hv
            best_c = cv
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

    rem = deadline - time.perf_counter()
    if rem > 30.0:
        h_fin, c_fin = pair_transfer_polish(
            best_h, deadline,
            top_frac=0.035,
            alpha_base=8.0,
            pair_pool=5,
            max_candidates=18,
            recompute_every=600
        )
        if c_fin < best_c:
            best_h = h_fin
            best_c = c_fin
            best_n = int(best_h.size)

    final_h = reject_or_none = project_density(best_h, 0.5 * int(best_h.size))
    final_c = eval_c5(final_h)
    if final_c <= best_c:
        best_h = final_h
        best_c = final_c
    else:
        final_h = project_density(best_h, 0.5 * int(best_h.size))
        final_c = eval_c5(final_h)
        best_h = final_h
        best_c = final_c

    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_h.size)
```
